To make bubble solution, you'll need a few simple ingredients and a few basic tools. Here's a step-by-step guide:

Ingredients:
- 1/2 cup of distilled water
- 1/2 cup of glycerin
- 1/4 cup of rubbing alcohol
- 1/4 cup of food coloring (optional)
- 1/4 cup of dish soap

Tools:
- A large bowl or a plastic container
- A measuring cup
- A mixing spoon

Instructions:

5. Finally, add 1/4 cup of dish soap to the bowl and mix until fully combined.
6. Transfer the bubble solution to a plastic container and label it with the ingredients and date.
7. Store the bubble solution in a cool, dark place for up to 2 weeks.

Your homemade bubble solution is now ready to use! Simply add a few drops of the solution to a bubble wand or a straw and blow to create beautiful, colorful bubbles.

2. **Beat the butter**: Beat the softened butter with a handheld mixer until it's light and creamy.
3. **Add the crisco crumbs**: Gradually add the crumbled crisco to the beaten butter and mix until it's well combined.
4. **Make the frosting**: Follow the regular recipe for your desired frosting (e.g., buttercream frosting, whipped cream) using the crisco-butter mixture.
5. **Adjust the recipe**: Since crisco is denser, you may need to add more sugar or milk to achieve the desired consistency.
6. **Bake the frosting**: Bake the frosting according to the recipe, keeping in mind that it may take longer to set, as crisco is denser.

The Wright brothers' achievement involved several key innovations, including:

1. **Experimentation and research**: They conducted extensive experiments with lightweight models, gliders, and even kites to understand the principles of flight and control.
2. **Innovative design**: They designed a lightweight, wooden frame with a unique wing shape that allowed for better lift and control.
3. **Power system**: They used a lightweight, gasoline engine, which was relatively new at the time.
4. **Control system**: They developed a sophisticated control system, using their wings to steer and ailerons to control roll.
5. **Aerodynamics**: They focused on reducing drag and increasing lift, which was crucial for achieving a successful powered flight.

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:

please explain the principals behind Marxism Long time lurker on reddit , first time poster :) Explain like I'm five.
Perplexity (WM): 6.3297 | Perplexity (No WM): 6.7516
Watermarked Text Detection: False Negative
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Marxism is an economic theory and political philosophy developed by Karl Marx and Friedrich Engels in the 19th century. The core principles of Marxism include:

1. **Marxist materialism**: Marxists believe that society is shaped by the means of production, including land, factories, and technology. The means of production determine the type of society that exists.

2. **Class struggle**: Marxists argue that society is divided into two classes: the proletariat (the working class) and the bourgeoisie (the ruling class). The proletariat is in constant conflict with the bourgeoisie, leading to a revolution towards a socialist society.

3. **Communism**: Marxists aim for a communist society, where there is no private property, no classes, and everyone has equal access to goods, services, and opportunities.

4. **Proletarian revolution**: Marxists believe that a revolution is necessary to overthrow the bourgeoisie and establish a proletarian dictatorship, where the proletariat takes control of the means of production.

5. **Critique capitalism**: Marxists argue that capitalism is inherently flawed, as it leads to inequality, exploitation, and environmental degradation.

In summary, Marxism aims to create a fair, equal, and just society where everyone has access to the means of production, opportunities, and well-being, and where there is no class struggle, private property, or exploitation.

[Original Text]
Marxism is a political theory developed by Karl Marx and Friedrich Engels in the 19th century. The main principles of Marxism are:

1. **Historical Materialism**: Marx believed that human history is shaped by economic forces, particularly the means of production. He argued that the economic structure of a society determines its social and political development.

2. **Class Struggle**: Marx saw society as divided into two main classes: the capitalist class, which owns the means of production, and the working class, which works them. He believed that the struggle between these two classes is the driving force of social change.

3. **Proletarian Emancipation**: Marx argued that the working class, or proletariat, must rise up and overthrow the capitalist class to establish a classless society. This is known as the concept of the proletarian revolution.

4. **Historical Communism**: Marx believed that capitalism is inherently unstable and will eventually lead to its collapse. He argued that the proletariat will rise up and establish a classless, communist society.

5. **Internationalism**: Marx believed that the proletariat must unite across national borders to establish a global communist revolution. He argued that nationalistic sentiments are secondary to the global struggle against capitalism.

Overall, Marxism is a theory that seeks to understand the economic and social forces that shape human history and aims to establish a classless, communist society.

Watermarked Text Detection: False Negative
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Binary is a way of writing numbers using only two symbols: 0 and 1. It's like a secret code that computers use to store information.

To read or write binary, you start with a certain number of digits. For example, if you want to write a number in binary with 4 digits, you would write 1010.

To read binary, you start from the rightmost digit, which is the least significant digit. Each digit represents a power of 2, from 2^0 (rightmost digit) to 2^3 (leftmost digit).

For example, the binary number 1010 can be read as follows:

- 0 x 2^0 = 0
- 1 x 2^1 = 2
- 0 x 2^2 = 0
- 1 x 2^3 = 8

So, the binary number 1010 is equal to 10 in decimal.

To write binary, you start from the least significant digit (the rightmost digit) and move left to the more significant digits. For example, if you want to write the number 10 in binary, you would write 1010.

Remember, binary is like a secret code using only two symbols, 0 and 1, and computers use this code to store and process information.

[Original Text]
Binary is a special way of writing numbers using only two digits: 0 and 1. It's like having a special language with only two words: "no" and "yes."

To read or write binary, you start from the rightmost digit (the first one on the right). Each digit represents a power of 2. For example:

- The rightmost digit is 2^0, which equals 1.
- The next digit to the left is 2^1, which equals 2.
- The next digit to the left is 2^2, which equals 4.
- And so on.

So, if you have a binary number like 1011, you can read it as:

- 1 x 2^3 + 0 x 2^2 + 1 x 2^1 + 1 x 2^0 = 8 + 0 + 2 + 1 = 11

This means that the binary number 1011 is equal to the decimal number 11.

To write a binary number, you just start from the rightmost digit and move to the left, with each digit representing a power of 2.

Remember, binary is just another way of writing numbers, and it's used by computers to process information. It's like having a secret code that only computers understand!

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:

Are capitalization rate and net profit margin the same thing?
Perplexity (WM): 4.2945 | Perplexity (No WM): 8.1429
Watermarked Text Detection: False Negative
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
No, capitalization rate and net profit margin are not the same thing. They are two distinct measures used to evaluate a company's financial performance.

Capitalization rate, also known as the return on investment (ROI), is a measure of a company's profitability relative to its total invested assets. It is calculated by dividing the company's net income by its total assets, and then multiplying by 100 to get a percentage. The capitalization rate indicates how much profit a company is generating relative to the total amount invested in its assets.

Net profit margin, on the other hand, a measure of a company's profitability relative to its total revenue. It is calculated by dividing the company's net income by its total revenue, and then multiplying by 100 to get a percentage. The net profit margin indicates how much profit a company is generating relative to its total revenue.

While both capitalization rate and net profit margin provide valuable insights into a company's profitability, they are calculated differently and serve different purposes. Capitalization rate focuses on the return earned on invested assets, whereas net profit margin focuses on the amount of profit generated relative to revenue.

In summary, capitalization rate and net profit margin are related concepts, but they are not the same thing. They are distinct measures used to evaluate a company's financial performance, with capitalization rate providing a measure of the return earned on invested assets, and net profit margin providing a measure of the profit generated relative to revenue.

[Original Text]
No, capitalization rate and net profit margin are not the same thing. The capitalization rate, also known as the dividend yield, is a measure of the return on a company's stock based on its dividend payments. It is calculated by dividing the annual dividend payment by the current stock price. On the other hand, the net profit margin is a measure of a company's ability to generate net profit from its revenue. It is calculated by dividing the net profit by the total revenue. While both metrics are related to a company's financial performance, they measure different aspects of profitability.

To defrost a refrigerator freezer effectively, follow these steps:

1. **Unplug the Refrigerator:** Remove the power source to prevent the freezer from running during the defrost process.
2. **Remove Contents:** Take out all items from the freezer to protect them from moisture and potential damage.
3. **Defrost Methods:**
   - **Natural Defrost:** Allow the freezer to defrost naturally. This can take a few hours, depending on the freezer.
   - **Manual Defrost:** Use a manual defrost tool or ice scraper to gently remove the ice.
   - **Water Defrost:** Fill a tray with water and place it inside the freezer. As the ice melts, the water will flow into the tray, making it easier to remove.
4. **Dry the Freezer:** Once the defrost process is complete, thoroughly dry the freezer interior with a clean towel or a hairdryer on a low setting.
5. **Install Filters:** Replace or clean filters, if necessary, to ensure proper airflow and optimal performance.
6. **Replug and Test:** Place items back into the freezer and plug it back in. Test the freezer to ensure it is functioning properly.
7. **Monitor Regularly:** Check for ice buildup and schedule regular defrosting to maintain optimal performance.

By following these steps, you can effectively defrost your refrigerator freezer, ensuring it operates efficiently and maintains the desired temperature.

[Original Text]
To defrost a refrigerator freezer, follow these steps:

1. **Unplug and unplug**: Unplug the refrigerator and freezer to prevent electrical shock or damage.
2. **Remove all contents**: Take out all items from the freezer and store them in a safe location.
3. **Open the freezer**: Leave the freezer door open to allow the ice to melt.
4. **Defrosting methods**:
   - **Manual defrosting**: Use a scraper or a brush to remove the ice and frost from the freezer walls and shelves.
   - **Water defrosting**: Place a bowl of water inside the freezer and let it melt away the ice.
   - **Steam defrosting**: Fill a pot with water and place it inside the freezer. Cover the pot and let the steam melt the ice.
   - **Natural defrosting**: Leave the freezer door open for a day or two, allowing the ice to melt naturally.
5. **Clean the freezer**: Once the ice has melted, clean the freezer walls and shelves with a damp cloth.
6. **Refill the freezer**: Close the freezer door and refill it with contents.
7. **Plug back in**: Plug the refrigerator and freezer back in and turn them on.

Remember to defrost your freezer every 6-12 months, depending on its size and usage, to prevent ice buildup and keep your refrigeration system running efficiently.

Perplexity (WM): 6.0707 | Perplexity (No WM): 6.6633
Watermarked Text Detection: False Negative
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Certainly! Vitel toné is a traditional Uruguayan soup made with beef broth, beef, vegetables, and spices. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 2 lbs ground beef (beef or lamb)
- 4 cups beef broth
- 1 large onion, chopped
- 2 cloves garlic, minced
- 2 large carrots, chopped
- 2 celery stalks, chopped
- 10 bay leaves
- 1 tsp chili powder
- 1 tsp ground cumin
- 1/2 tsp paprika
- Salt and pepper to taste

Instructions:
1. In a large pot, sauté the onion, garlic, carrots, and celery with a bit of olive oil until softened.
2. Add the ground beef and cook until browned.
3. Stir in the bay leaves, chili powder, ground cumin, and paprika.
4. Add the beef broth and bring to a boil.
5. Reduce the heat to low, cover, and simmer for 20-30 minutes, or until the beef is tender.
6. Remove the bay leaves and season with salt and pepper to taste.
7. Ladle the soup into bowls and garnish with chopped parsley, if desired.

This soup is hearty and comforting, perfect for a cozy night in. Serve it with some crusty bread or on the side for a complete meal. Enjoy!

[Original Text]
Certainly! Vitel toné is a classic Uruguayan dish that's sure to impress your guests. Here's a recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 1 large white fish, sliced
- 1 large onion, chopped
- 2 cloves garlic, minced
- 1 bell pepper, chopped
- 1 red tomato, chopped
- 1 cup white wine
- 1 cup vegetable broth
- 1 cup chicken broth
- 1 tbsp olive oil
- Salt and pepper to taste
- Fresh parsley, chopped for garnish

Instructions:
1. Slice the fish into bite-sized pieces.
2. In a large pot, heat the olive oil over medium heat.
3. Add the chopped onion, garlic, bell pepper, and tomato. Sauté until softened.
4. Pour in the white wine, vegetable broth, and chicken broth. Bring to a boil.
5. Add the sliced fish to the pot and reduce to a simmer.
6. Cover the pot and let it cook for 15-20 minutes, or until the fish is cooked through.
7. Season with salt and pepper to taste.
8. Garnish with chopped parsley and serve hot.

This dish is often served with rice or potatoes, and it's perfect for a hearty and flavorful meal. I hope you find this recipe helpful and enjoy Vitel toné with your guests!

Ingredients:
- 4-6 whole chicken breasts
- 2 cups coconut milk
- 1 large onion, chopped
- 3 cloves garlic, minced
- 1 celery stalk, sliced
- 1 carrot, sliced
- 1/2 teaspoon cumin powder
- 1/2 teaspoon turmeric
- 1/2 teaspoon salt
- 1/4 teaspoon black pepper
- 1 tablespoon turmeric paste
- 1/4 cup vegetable oil

Instructions:

2. Add the chopped onion and cook until softened, about 5 minutes.
3. Add the garlic and cook for another minute.
4. Add the sliced celery, carrot, and turmeric paste. Cook for another minute.
5. Add the coconut milk, cumin powder, turmeric, salt, and black pepper. Stir well.

Sure! Opor Ayam is a delicious Indonesian dish that's perfect for hosting guests. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 1 whole chicken
- Salt and pepper to taste
- 2 large onions, chopped
- 4 cloves garlic, minced
- 1-inch ginger, grated
- 1/4 cup coconut milk
- 1/4 cup soy sauce
- 1/4 cup brown sugar
- 1/4 cup palm sugar
- 1/4 cup fish sauce
- 1/4 cup vegetable oil
- 2 tablespoons shrimp paste
- 1 tablespoon curry powder
- 1 tablespoon lemongrass
- 1/4 cup water

Instructions:

2. In a large pot, heat the oil over medium heat.
3. Add the onions, garlic, and ginger and cook until softened.
4. Add the shrimp paste, curry powder, and lemongrass, and cook for 1 minute.
5. Add the coconut milk, soy sauce, brown sugar, palm sugar, fish sauce, and water to the pot.
6. Add the chicken to the pot and bring to a boil.
7. Reduce the heat and let it simmer for 30-40 minutes or until the chicken is tender.

Enjoy your delicious Opor Ayam!

What do the different octane values of gasoline mean?
Perplexity (WM): 9.0768 | Perplexity (No WM): 6.3467
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
The octane value of gasoline is a measure of the fuel's ability to resist autoignition in an engine. This resistance is crucial for preventing engine knocking, which can damage the engine and reduce performance. Higher octane values mean the fuel is less prone to premature combustion, resulting in smoother running and better performance.

There are two main octane ratings for gasoline:

1. **Research Octane (RON)**: This is the standard rating for US gasoline, based on the US Department of Energy's (DOE) Research Octane test, which measures the resistance of a fuel mixture to autoignition. The rating ranges from 71 to 98, with a higher number indicating a higher octane value.

2. **Research Octane (RON)**: This rating is used in other countries and is based on the European Energy Agency's (EA) Research Octane test. The rating ranges from 85 to 95.

3. **Japanese Octane Number (JON)**: This rating is used in Japan and is based on the Japanese Energy Agency's (JEA) Research Octane test. The rating ranges from 86 to 94.

Overall, higher octane values mean better engine performance and reduced risk of engine knocking and damage. However, higher octane fuels can also be more expensive, and older engines may be able to run smoothly with lower octane fuels.

[Original Text]
The octane value of gasoline is a measure of the fuel's ability to resist engine knocking, also known as detonation. This occurs when the combustion process starts before the spark plug can ignite it, causing the engine to produce a knocking sound. A higher octane value indicates that the fuel is more resistant to detonation, while a lower octane value means that the fuel is more prone to it.

There are two main types of octane ratings:

1. ** cetane rating**: This is a more modern and widely used measure of octane rating. It is based on the cetane index, which is a measure of the fuel's ability to resist detonation under laboratory conditions. The cetane rating is typically given as a range from 90 to 100.

2. ** Octane rating**: This is an older measure of octane rating, which is based on the octane index. The octane rating is typically given as a range from 90 to 100.

A higher octane value means that the fuel is more resistant to detonation, while a lower octane value means that the fuel is more prone to it. The octane value of gasoline can vary depending on the type of engine, the driving conditions, and the specific fuel being used. It's important to use the correct octane value of gasoline for your vehicle's engine to ensure optimal performance and prevent damage to the engine.

Are RSUs ever taxed as long term capital gains?

Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Yes, restricted stock units (RSUs) can be taxed as long-term capital gains under certain conditions. RSUs are typically issued by a company to employees, which grants the employee the right to purchase shares of the company at a predetermined price within a certain period, usually 1-5 years.

When RSUs are sold before the expiration of the grant, the gain is generally treated as a short-term capital gain, which is taxed at a lower tax rate. However, if the RSUs are sold after the expiration of the grant, the gain can be treated as a long-term capital gain, which is taxed at a higher tax rate.

In some cases, the employee may hold RSUs for a specific period, such as a year or more, before selling them. In such cases, the gain may be treated as a long-term capital gain.

It's essential to note that selling RSUs before the expiration of the grant can also result in a capital loss, which can be used to offset capital gains from other investments.

Overall, RSUs can be taxed as long-term capital gains when the shares are held after the expiration of the grant or for a specific period before being sold.

[Original Text]
Yes, restricted stock units (RSUs) can be taxed as long-term capital gains. RSUs are a type of vesting plan where employees receive shares of a company's stock in exchange for working for the company. When an employee sells their RSUs, the gain is treated as a long-term capital gain, which is subject to ordinary income tax rates.

However, it's important to note that RSUs are generally subject to tax benefits that do not apply to long-term capital gains. For example, RSUs may be subject to the RSU tax credit, which is a tax credit that can be used to offset income taxes owed. Additionally, RSUs may be subject to the RSU tax exemption, which is a tax exemption that can be used to reduce the tax liability associated with selling RSUs.

It's also worth noting that RSUs are generally not subject to the long-term tax deduction, which is a tax deduction that applies to long-term capital gains held for at least one year. Instead, RSUs are generally subject to the short-term tax deduction, which is a tax deduction that applies to short-term capital gains held for less than one year.

Overall, while RSUs can be taxed as long-term capital gains, they are generally subject to special tax benefits and exemptions that do not apply to long-term capital gains. It's important to consult with a tax professional to understand the specific tax benefits and exemptions that may apply to your individual situation.

Certainly! Milanesa de peceto is a traditional Argentinian dish made with beef, onions, and spices. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 2 pounds of beef, cut into small cubes
- 2 large onions, chopped
- 1 clove garlic, minced
- 1 cup of chopped tomatoes
- 1 cup of chopped carrots
- 1 cup of chopped celery
- 1 cup of vegetable broth
- 1 teaspoon of ground cumin
- 1 teaspoon of smoked paprika
- Salt and pepper to taste

Instructions:
1. In a large pot, sauté the chopped onions and garlic over medium heat until softened.
2. Add the beef cubes and cook until browned on all sides.
3. Stir in the chopped tomatoes, carrots, and celery.
4. Pour in the vegetable broth, ground cumin, and smoked paprika.
5. Bring the mixture to a boil, then reduce the heat to low and simmer for 20-25 minutes, or until the beef is tender.
6. Season with salt and pepper to taste.
7. Serve the Milanesa de peceto hot, garnished with fresh cilantro if desired.

This dish is often served with rice or potatoes, and it's a great way to introduce your guests to Argentinian cuisine. The hearty flavors and tender beef cubes are sure to impress your guests!

Kevlar is made up of a chain of polymer chains, which are long molecules composed of repeating units called monomers. The monomers are connected by a chemical bond called a peptide bond, which links two amino acids together. In the case of polyamide-6,6, the monomers are polyamides, which are a type of amino acid.

The polymer chains in Kevlar are arranged in a regular, crystalline structure, which gives the fiber its strength and stiffness. This crystalline structure allows Kevlar to maintain its shape, even when it is stretched or bent, making it a unique material for high-performance applications.

Kevlar is known for its high tensile strength, stiffness, and toughness, making it an ideal material for applications such as sports equipment, body armor, textiles, and even in the construction of space vehicles. Kevlar, also known as KevlarTM, is a synthetic fiber made from a polyamide polymer. The production of Kevlar involves a complex process that involves the polymerization of two monomers, 2,6-diaminophenylenediamine (DAP) and 2,6-diaminophenylenediamide (DID). These monomers are linked together to form a long chain of repeating units, which is the basis of the Kevlar fiber. The Kevlar fiber is then spun into a thin, flexible thread through a process called electrospinning, which involves the use of an electric field to draw the polymer solution into a thin thread. The resulting thread is then woven into a fabric, which can be used in a variety of applications, including protective gear, clothing, and industrial materials. Kevlar is known for its exceptional strength, durability, and chemical resistance, making it an ideal material for use in applications where high performance is required.

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:
Hi, I'd like to learn to bake challah. Can you give me a recipe for that?
Perplexity (WM): 4.8862 | Perplexity (No WM): 4.2626
Watermarked Text Detection: False Negative
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Sure! Here's a recipe for a classic challah:

Ingredients:
- 4 cups all-purpose flour
- 1 cup granulated sugar
- 1/2 cup vegetable oil
- 1/2 cup warm water
- 1 tablespoon salt
- 2 teaspoons yeast
- 1/4 teaspoon salt

Instructions:
1. Preheat your oven to 375°F (190°C).
2. In a large bowl, combine the flour, sugar, and vegetable oil.
3. Add the warm water, salt, and yeast. Mix until a dough forms.
4. Kne the dough on a floured surface for about 10-15 minutes until it becomes smooth and elastic.
5. Place the dough in a greased bowl and let it rise in a warm place for about 1 hour, or until it has doubled in size.
6. Once the dough has risen, sprinkle the remaining salt all over the dough and mix it in.
7. Shape the dough into a rectangle, about 12 inches long and 6 inches wide.
8. Place the challah on a baking sheet lined with parchment paper.
9. Bake in the preheated oven for 30-40 minutes, or until the top is golden brown and the bottom is set.
10. Allow the challah to cool for 10 minutes before slicing.

[Original Text]
Sure! Here's a simple challah recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 2 cups all-purpose flour
- 1 cup butter, softened
- 1 cup eggs
- 1/2 teaspoon salt
- 1/2 teaspoon baking powder
- 1/2 teaspoon baking soda
- 1/2 teaspoon sugar
- 1/2 teaspoon vanilla extract

Instructions:
1. Preheat your oven to 350°F (175°C).
2. In a large mixing bowl, combine the flour, butter, salt, baking powder, and baking soda.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk together the eggs, sugar, and vanilla extract.
4. Pour the wet ingredients into the dry ingredients and mix until just combined.
5. Shape the dough into a ball and let it rest at room temperature for about 30 minutes.
6. Divide the dough into 4 equal pieces and roll each piece into a circle, about 1 inch thick.
7. Place the circles on a baking sheet lined with parchment paper.
8. Bake the challah in the preheated oven for 35-40 minutes, or until golden brown.
9. Let the challah cool for a few minutes before slicing and serving.

This recipe makes a delicious challah that's perfect for serving with a special meal or enjoying as a snack. Happy baking!

Tong Sui, also known as Noodle Soup, is a classic Hong Kong dish that's sure to impress your guests. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 2 oz egg noodles (or your preferred type)
- 4 cups beef broth
- 1 onion, sliced
- 2 cloves garlic, minced
- 1 bell pepper, sliced
- 1 celery stalk, sliced
- 1 tbsp vegetable oil
- 1 tbsp soy sauce
- 1 tbsp rice vinegar
- 1 tbsp brown sugar
- 1 tsp sesame oil
- Sesame seeds (optional)

Instructions:

2. Add the onion, garlic, bell pepper, and celery, and sauté for 2-3 minutes until softened.
3. Add the noodles, beef broth, soy sauce, rice vinegar, brown sugar, and sesame oil to the pot.
4. Bring the mixture to a boil, then reduce the heat to low and simmer for 10-15 minutes, or until the noodles are cooked through.
5. Sprinkle with sesame seeds (if using) and serve hot.

- 1 tsp sugar
- 1 tsp garlic powder
- 1 tsp sesame oil

In a vacuum, a feather, a coin, and a bowling ball fall at exactly the same speed because there is no air resistance slowing them down. Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:
Is human blood ever any color other than red?
Perplexity (WM): 6.3112 | Perplexity (No WM): 7.2105
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Human blood is typically red in color due to the presence of hemoglobin, a protein found in red blood cells that carries oxygen throughout the body. However, in rare cases, human blood can take on colors other than red.

One such condition is hemolytic anemia, a blood disorder where the body's red blood cells break down more quickly than usual. As a result, the hemoglobin is released into the bloodstream, which can cause the blood to appear pale or bluish in color, depending on the amount of hemoglobin released.

Another example is congenital methemoglobinemia, a rare blood disorder where the body produces an abnormal form of hemoglobin called methemoglobin. Methemoglobin has a dark, blue-black color, which can cause the blood to appear blue or green.

In some cases, the blood can also appear yellow or greenish due to the presence of bilirubin, a yellow pigment produced during the breakdown of red blood cells. This condition is known as hemolytic jaundice.

However, it's essential to note that these conditions are relatively rare and can cause significant health problems, including serious complications, organ damage, and even death.

In summary, while human blood is typically red in color, in rare cases, it can take on colors other than red due to blood disorders such as hemolytic anemia, congenital methemoglobinemia, or hemolytic jaundice. These conditions can be serious and require prompt medical attention.

[Original Text]
Human blood is typically red due to the presence of hemoglobin, a protein in red blood cells that carries oxygen. However, there are rare cases where blood can appear different colors. For example, some individuals may have blue or green blood due to an abnormal type of hemoglobin called methemoglobin. This condition occurs when the hemoglobin is converted to a compound called methemoglobin, which has a blue or green color.

Other rare conditions can cause blood to appear brown or black. For instance, in some cases, the hemoglobin can become oxidized and turn a brown or black color. This is often referred to as "blood rust" or "blood discoloration."

In rare cases, blood can also appear yellow or green due to the presence of bilirubin, a yellow pigment produced during the breakdown of red blood cells. This condition, known as hemolytic anemia, can cause the blood to appear yellow or green.

It's worth noting that the color of blood can also be influenced by other factors, such as the presence of certain substances in the blood or the conditions under which it is observed. For example, if the blood is exposed to oxygen or other chemicals, it can darken or change color.

In summary, while human blood is typically red, there are rare cases where it can appear different colors, including blue, green, brown, black, yellow, or green. These variations can occur due to abnormal hemoglobin, oxidation, or other medical conditions.

Imagine you're trying to draw a picture of a tiny, tiny thing called a tiny atom. Atoms are so small, we can't see them with our eyes. But scientists use special tools called microscopes to look at them. 

Microscopes are like super powerful magnifying glasses. They help us see things so small, they can't be seen with the naked eye. When we look at an atom under a microscope, we can see that it's made up of even smaller parts called protons, neutrons, and electrons. 

Protons are like tiny positive balls that sit in the center of the atom. Neutrons are like tiny balls that have no charge and are also in the center. Electrons are like tiny, negative balls that orbit around the center of the atom. 

So, when we look at an atom under a microscope, we can see that it's like a tiny, tiny ball with all these protons, neutrons, and electrons inside. It's like a tiny, tiny ball of tiny balls! 

So, while we can't see atoms with our eyes, we can see them under a microscope and know what they look like. It's not like we're just guessing; we can actually see them!

Why can't you just have someone invest for you and split the profits (and losses) with him?

[Watermarked Text]
While it may seem convenient to have someone else invest for you and split the profits and losses, there are several reasons why this approach is not advisable.

1. **Lack of expertise**: Many individuals may not possess the necessary knowledge or experience to make informed investment decisions. This can lead to poor choices, potential losses, or even legal issues.

2. **Lack of accountability**: When someone else invests on your behalf, they may not be accountable for the outcome. This can lead to a lack of transparency, potential misunderstandings, or even legal disputes.

3. **Financial risks**: When someone else invests for you, they may not be aware of the financial risks involved. This can lead to unintended consequences, financial losses, or even legal issues.

4. **Conflict of interest**: When someone else invests on your behalf, they may not be acting in your best interest. This can lead to conflicts of interest, potential losses, or even legal issues.

5. **Tax implications**: When someone else invests on your behalf, they may have different tax obligations, which can lead to unexpected costs or legal issues.

6. **Limited control**: When someone else invests for you, they may not have the authority to make informed investment decisions. This can lead to limited control, potential losses, or even legal issues.

It's essential to make your own investment decisions and consult with a financial advisor before having someone else invest on your behalf.

[Original Text]
While it may seem convenient to have someone invest for you and split the profits and losses, there are several reasons why this approach is not advisable. Firstly, investing requires a certain level of knowledge, expertise, and risk management skills that many people may not possess. By relying on someone else's investment decisions, you may be putting your money at risk.

Secondly, investing can be complex, and not everyone has the necessary training or experience to make informed decisions. If someone else is managing your investments, they may not be able to understand the nuances of the market or the specific requirements of your financial goals. This can lead to poor investment decisions and potential losses.

Thirdly, having someone invest for you can be a conflict of interest. The person managing your investments may not always have your best interests at heart, and may be motivated by their own profits rather than your financial goals. This can lead to suboptimal investment decisions and potential losses.

Lastly, having someone invest for you can also be a breach of trust. Investing requires a certain level of trust in the person managing your investments, and if that person is not trustworthy or reliable, you may be putting your money at risk.

In summary, having someone invest for you and split the profits and losses is not advisable due to the potential risks, complexities, conflicts of interest, and breaches of trust. It is essential to invest in your own education and seek advice from qualified professionals to make informed investment decisions.
